    OPT   ALU is the highest-utilized pipeline (62.3%) based on elapsed cycles in the workload, taking into account the 
          rates of its different instructions. It executes integer and logic operations. The pipeline is                
          well-utilized, but might become a bottleneck if more work is added. Based on the number of executed           
          instructions, the highest utilized pipeline (62.3%) is ALU. It executes integer and logic operations.         
          Comparing the two, the overall pipeline utilization appears to be caused by frequent, low-latency             
          instructions. See the Profiling Guide                                                                         
          (https://docs.nvidia.com/nsight-compute/ProfilingGuide/index.html#metrics-decoder) or hover over the          
          pipeline name to understand the workloads handled by each pipeline. The Instruction Statistics section shows  
          the mix of executed instructions for this workload. Check the Warp State Statistics section for which         
          reasons cause warps to stall.

    OPT   Est. Local Speedup: 62.41%                                                                                    
          The difference between calculated theoretical (100.0%) and measured achieved occupancy (37.6%) can be the     
          result of warp scheduling overheads or workload imbalances during the kernel execution. Load imbalances can   
          occur between warps within a block as well as across blocks of the same kernel. See the CUDA Best Practices   
          Guide (https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-c-best-practices-guide/index.html#occupancy) for more details on     
          optimizing occupancy.

    OPT   Est. Local Speedup: 41.42%                                                                                    
          Every scheduler is capable of issuing one instruction per cycle, but for this workload each scheduler only    
          issues an instruction every 1.7 cycles. This might leave hardware resources underutilized and may lead to     
          less optimal performance. Out of the maximum of 16 warps per scheduler, this workload allocates an average    
          of 7.39 active warps per scheduler, but only an average of 1.38 warps were eligible per cycle. Eligible       
          warps are the subset of active warps that are ready to issue their next instruction. Every cycle with no      
          eligible warp results in no instruction being issued and the issue slot remains unused. To increase the       
          number of eligible warps, reduce the time the active warps are stalled by inspecting the top stall reasons    
          on the Warp State Statistics and Source Counters sections.

    OPT   Est. Local Speedup: 43.8%                                                                                     
          Every scheduler is capable of issuing one instruction per cycle, but for this workload each scheduler only    
          issues an instruction every 1.8 cycles. This might leave hardware resources underutilized and may lead to     
          less optimal performance. Out of the maximum of 16 warps per scheduler, this workload allocates an average    
          of 7.12 active warps per scheduler, but only an average of 1.33 warps were eligible per cycle. Eligible       
          warps are the subset of active warps that are ready to issue their next instruction. Every cycle with no      
          eligible warp results in no instruction being issued and the issue slot remains unused. To increase the       
          number of eligible warps, reduce the time the active warps are stalled by inspecting the top stall reasons    
          on the Warp State Statistics and Source Counters sections.

    OPT   Est. Local Speedup: 44.92%                                                                                    
          Every scheduler is capable of issuing one instruction per cycle, but for this workload each scheduler only    
          issues an instruction every 1.8 cycles. This might leave hardware resources underutilized and may lead to     
          less optimal performance. Out of the maximum of 16 warps per scheduler, this workload allocates an average    
          of 7.07 active warps per scheduler, but only an average of 1.30 warps were eligible per cycle. Eligible       
          warps are the subset of active warps that are ready to issue their next instruction. Every cycle with no      
          eligible warp results in no instruction being issued and the issue slot remains unused. To increase the       
          number of eligible warps, reduce the time the active warps are stalled by inspecting the top stall reasons    
          on the Warp State Statistics and Source Counters sections.

    OPT   Est. Local Speedup: 41.41%                                                                                    
          Every scheduler is capable of issuing one instruction per cycle, but for this workload each scheduler only    
          issues an instruction every 1.7 cycles. This might leave hardware resources underutilized and may lead to     
          less optimal performance. Out of the maximum of 16 warps per scheduler, this workload allocates an average    
          of 7.38 active warps per scheduler, but only an average of 1.37 warps were eligible per cycle. Eligible       
          warps are the subset of active warps that are ready to issue their next instruction. Every cycle with no      
          eligible warp results in no instruction being issued and the issue slot remains unused. To increase the       
          number of eligible warps, reduce the time the active warps are stalled by inspecting the top stall reasons    
          on the Warp State Statistics and Source Counters sections.

    OPT   ALU is the highest-utilized pipeline (64.6%) based on elapsed cycles in the workload, taking into account the 
          rates of its different instructions. It executes integer and logic operations. The pipeline is                
          well-utilized, but might become a bottleneck if more work is added. Based on the number of executed           
          instructions, the highest utilized pipeline (64.6%) is ALU. It executes integer and logic operations.         
          Comparing the two, the overall pipeline utilization appears to be caused by frequent, low-latency             
          instructions. See the Profiling Guide                                                                         
          (https://docs.nvidia.com/nsight-compute/ProfilingGuide/index.html#metrics-decoder) or hover over the          
          pipeline name to understand the workloads handled by each pipeline. The Instruction Statistics section shows  
          the mix of executed instructions for this workload. Check the Warp State Statistics section for which         
          reasons cause warps to stall.

    OPT   Est. Local Speedup: 43.2%                                                                                     
          Every scheduler is capable of issuing one instruction per cycle, but for this workload each scheduler only    
          issues an instruction every 1.8 cycles. This might leave hardware resources underutilized and may lead to     
          less optimal performance. Out of the maximum of 16 warps per scheduler, this workload allocates an average    
          of 7.38 active warps per scheduler, but only an average of 0.83 warps were eligible per cycle. Eligible       
          warps are the subset of active warps that are ready to issue their next instruction. Every cycle with no      
          eligible warp results in no instruction being issued and the issue slot remains unused. To increase the       
          number of eligible warps, reduce the time the active warps are stalled by inspecting the top stall reasons    
          on the Warp State Statistics and Source Counters sections.

    OPT   Est. Local Speedup: 44.13%                                                                                    
          Every scheduler is capable of issuing one instruction per cycle, but for this workload each scheduler only    
          issues an instruction every 1.8 cycles. This might leave hardware resources underutilized and may lead to     
          less optimal performance. Out of the maximum of 16 warps per scheduler, this workload allocates an average    
          of 7.26 active warps per scheduler, but only an average of 0.82 warps were eligible per cycle. Eligible       
          warps are the subset of active warps that are ready to issue their next instruction. Every cycle with no      
          eligible warp results in no instruction being issued and the issue slot remains unused. To increase the       
          number of eligible warps, reduce the time the active warps are stalled by inspecting the top stall reasons    
          on the Warp State Statistics and Source Counters sections.

    Table Description : Pipeline utilization based on the number of cycles the pipeline was active. This takes the rates of different instructions executing on the pipeline into account.     OPT   Est. Local Speedup: 44.14%                                                                                    
          Every scheduler is capable of issuing one instruction per cycle, but for this workload each scheduler only    
          issues an instruction every 1.8 cycles. This might leave hardware resources underutilized and may lead to     
          less optimal performance. Out of the maximum of 16 warps per scheduler, this workload allocates an average    
          of 7.51 active warps per scheduler, but only an average of 1.23 warps were eligible per cycle. Eligible       
          warps are the subset of active warps that are ready to issue their next instruction. Every cycle with no      
          eligible warp results in no instruction being issued and the issue slot remains unused. To increase the       
          number of eligible warps, reduce the time the active warps are stalled by inspecting the top stall reasons    
          on the Warp State Statistics and Source Counters sections.

    OPT   Est. Speedup: 44.14%                                                                                          
          The difference between calculated theoretical (100.0%) and measured achieved occupancy (46.3%) can be the     
          result of warp scheduling overheads or workload imbalances during the kernel execution. Load imbalances can   
          occur between warps within a block as well as across blocks of the same kernel. See the CUDA Best Practices   
          Guide (https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-c-best-practices-guide/index.html#occupancy) for more details on     
          optimizing occupancy.
